TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter Weather December 2029: Seasonal Tips
DESCRIPTION: Plan your Marbella yacht charter in December 2029 with our seasonal weather guide. Average 16°C, 6h sun, and tips for a smooth trip. Book now.

Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter Weather December 2029: Seasonal Tips By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a Marbella yacht charter in December 2029 ? The Costa del Sol enjoys mild winters, but knowing the weather patterns helps you pack right and pick the perfect itinerary. Here’s everything you need to know about December conditions, sea state, and seasonal tips for a comfortable day on the water. December Weather in Marbella: The Numbers December in Marbella is cool but far from cold. Average daytime highs reach 17°C, with lows around 9°C. Sea temperature sits at 16°C – refreshing but not unbearable for a quick dip. You’ll get roughly 9.5 hours of daylight, with about 6 hours of direct sunshine. Rainfall averages 60 mm spread over 7 days, so a shower is possible but not guaranteed. Sea Conditions and Wind The Mediterranean stays calm in December. Swells rarely exceed 1.5 metres, and winds blow from the west at 10–15 knots. The sheltered waters of Puerto Banús and Marbella Marina are ideal for cruising. If you’re heading towards Estepona or Sotogrande, expect a slight chop but nothing that would trouble a 12-metre-plus yacht. Best Boats for December Charters For cooler days, choose a boat with an enclosed flybridge or heated interior. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) offers panoramic views from a sheltered helm. The Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) has a spacious salon with heating. For smaller groups, the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) or Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) both have cosy cabins. All charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, and snacks. What to Wear and Pack Layers are essential. Start with a thermal base, add a fleece or jumper, and top with a windproof jacket. Non-slip shoes, sunglasses, and SPF 30+ sunscreen are still recommended – the sun is low but UV reflects off the water. Bring a hat and gloves if you’re sensitive to cold. The skipper will have blankets and hot drinks available. Suggested December Itineraries A 4-hour charter from Puerto Banús can take you east to Cala del Faro or west to Cala Cortés – both sheltered coves with calm waters. For a longer trip, head to Cabopino or Estepona for lunch at a beachfront chiringuito. The Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) includes a free jet ski, adding excitement even in winter. Booking Tips for December 2029 Book early – December is popular for Christmas parties and corporate events. We recommend flexible dates in case of a storm. If the skipper deems conditions unsafe, you get a full refund or reschedule. Contact us via WhatsApp for bespoke quotes on boats like the Canados 86 or Maiora 26m. Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39) start at €749 for 2 hours, all inclusive. Frequently Asked Questions What is the average temperature in Marbella during December 2029? Daytime highs average 17°C, dropping to 9°C at night. Sea temperature is around 16°C, so swimming is possible but brisk. Pack a jacket and layers. How much sunshine can I expect on a December yacht charter? December offers about 6 hours of direct sunshine per day, with sunrise around 08:20 and sunset at 18:00. Morning trips maximise the best light. Is December a good month for a yacht charter in Marbella? Yes – it’s the off-season, so marinas are quieter and prices can be lower. The weather is mild enough for cruising, though swimming is less popular. Many clients enjoy coastal sightseeing and onboard dining. What should I wear on a December yacht charter in Marbella? Layers are key: a thermal base, fleece or jumper, and a windproof jacket. Non-slip shoes, sunglasses, and sunscreen are still recommended. The skipper will have blankets onboard. What is the sea state like in Marbella in December? Generally calm to slight, with swells of 0.5–1.5 metres. Winds are typically 10–15 knots from the west. The Strait of Gibraltar can be rougher, but sheltered bays like Puerto Banús remain protected. Can I cancel my December charter if the weather is bad? Yes – we monitor forecasts closely. If the skipper deems conditions unsafe, we offer a full refund or reschedule at no extra cost. We recommend booking flexible dates. Which boats are best for a December charter in Marbella? Enclosed flybridge or salon boats like the Azimut 58 Flybridge or Ferretti 94 provide shelter from the breeze. For smaller groups, the Azimut 39 or Astondoa 40 offer heated cabins. Do you offer special December packages or discounts? Our standard rates apply, but availability is higher in winter. Rain falls on roughly 6-8 days, usually as short showers. The wind is light, typically 5-10 knots from the west or northwest, making for calm seas within 5 nautical miles of the coast. TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter Weather in December: Winter Cruising Guide
DESCRIPTION: Discover marbella yacht charter weather december conditions: 15-19°C, 8h sun, calm seas. Plan your winter cruising with our guide and book your boat.

Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter Weather in December: Winter Cruising Guide By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Thinking about a marbella yacht charter weather december ? You might picture summer heat, but winter cruising on the Costa del Sol has its own quiet charm. With daytime highs of 15–19°C, calm seas, and far fewer boats, December offers a relaxed, uncrowded way to explore the coast from Puerto Banús. This guide covers what to expect—temperatures, wind, sea conditions, and how to plan your trip. December Weather Overview for Marbella Marbella’s winter is mild by European standards. Average daily maximums hover around 17–19°C, with overnight lows of 9–12°C. Sea temperatures sit at 16°C—cool but not freezing. You’ll get roughly 8 hours of daylight (sunrise ~08:15, sunset ~18:00). Rain is possible on 5–8 days, typically as short showers rather than all-day downpours. The Sierra Blanca mountains often hold a dusting of snow, adding a scenic backdrop. Sea Conditions: Swell, Wind, and Waves The Mediterranean is generally calm in December. Swell heights average under 0.5 metres, and wind speeds range from 5–15 knots. The dominant wind is the Poniente (west wind), which brings dry, settled weather. The Levante (east wind) can pick up occasionally, but your skipper will choose a sheltered route along the coast. For example, heading west towards Estepona or east to Cabopino keeps you in lee of the land. Our boats—from the Marbella yacht charter fleet—are all equipped for winter cruising, with heated cabins and enclosed flybridges on models like the Azimut 58 Flybridge. Best Times to Charter in December The most popular slots are midday to sunset (roughly 11:00–18:00). A 4-hour charter (e.g., 13:00–17:00) gives you ample time to cruise, enjoy lunch, and watch the sun dip behind La Concha. For a full day, an 8-hour charter from 10:00–18:00 lets you venture further—perhaps to Cala del Faro or even Sotogrande if conditions permit. Early mornings can be cooler (10–12°C), so many guests prefer a later start. What to Wear on a Winter Charter Layering is essential. Start with a thermal base layer, add a fleece or jumper, and top with a windproof jacket. Bring a hat, sunglasses, and sunscreen—the winter sun can still be strong, especially with reflection off the water. Closed-toe, non-slip shoes are recommended for deck safety. The boat’s cabin provides warmth, but you’ll want a jacket when outside. Recommended Boats for December Cruising All our boats operate year-round from Puerto Banús. For a small group, the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 (both €749/2h to €2,299/8h, skipper + drinks + VAT included) are cosy and efficient. For a larger party, the Mangusta 80 (€4,719 minimum 4h, includes a jet ski) offers spacious decks and a heated saloon. Other vessels—like the Ferretti 94 or Canados 86 —are available on request. All charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, soft drinks, beer, cava, wine, snacks, insurance, and 21% IVA. Winter Cruising Routes from Puerto Banús Popular December routes include: Marbella coastline – Cruise past the Golden Mile, Marbella Marina, and the old town, with mountain views. Cabopino – East of Marbella, this sheltered cove offers calm waters and a sandy beach (great for photos). Estepona – Westbound, you’ll pass the Río Verde and Cala Cortés, with the Sierra Bermeja as a backdrop. Sotogrande – A longer run (about 20 NM) for a full-day charter, with luxury marina views. Your skipper will adjust the route based on wind and swell to ensure a smooth ride. What’s Included in Your Winter Charter Every booking includes a licensed skipper, fuel, insurance, and a selection of drinks (water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava) plus light snacks. The boat is heated, and blankets are available on request. There’s no extra charge for winter cruising—the same prices apply year-round. Check individual boat pages for details or contact us via WhatsApp for a custom quote. Frequently Asked Questions Is December a good time for a yacht charter in Marbella? Yes, if you prefer mild temperatures and fewer crowds. Daytime highs of 15-19°C and calm seas make for pleasant cruising. It’s ideal for sightseeing, photography, and enjoying Marbella’s winter sun without the summer heat. What is the sea temperature in Marbella in December? The Mediterranean sea temperature around Marbella in December averages 16°C. Swimming is possible with a wetsuit, but most guests prefer to enjoy the views and onboard comforts rather than a dip. How windy is it in Marbella in December? Average wind speeds are 5-15 knots, with occasional stronger Levante or Poniente winds. Most days are calm enough for chartering, especially in the morning. Your skipper will advise on the best route based on conditions. What should I wear on a yacht charter in December? Layers are key: a long-sleeve base layer, fleece or jumper, and a windproof jacket. Bring a hat and sunglasses for the sun, plus closed-toe shoes with non-slip soles. The boat’s cabin provides warmth if needed. Can I see the sunset on a December charter? Absolutely. Sunset in Marbella in December is around 18:00, so an afternoon charter from 14:00-18:00 or 15:00-19:00 is perfect for watching the sun dip behind La Concha and the Mediterranean. Do all boats operate in December? Yes, all boats in our fleet depart from Puerto Banús year-round, including the Astondoa 40, Azimut 39, Mangusta 80, and others. Check availability on the boat page or message us for specific dates. What routes are recommended in December? Popular winter routes include cruising along the Marbella coastline to Cabopino or heading west to Estepona. These sheltered waters offer scenic views and calm conditions, with plenty of photo opportunities of the Sierra Blanca mountains. Is it worth booking a full-day charter in December? An 8-hour charter (e.g., 10:00-18:00) gives you the full winter day, including lunch on board and sunset. With fewer daylight hours, a 4-6 hour trip is often enough to enjoy the coast and return before dark. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

February Weather Snapshot for Marbella February is one of Marbella's cooler months, but it's far from bleak. Puerto Banús to Cala del Faro – 30 minutes, a sheltered cove perfect for a picnic. Puerto Banús to Estepona – 1 hour, with views of La Concha mountain. TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter in February: Weather, Tips, and Deals
DESCRIPTION: Plan a Marbella yacht charter in February with our guide to weather, sea conditions, and off-peak deals. Average 16°C, calm seas, and lower rates.

Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter in February: Weather, Tips, and Deals By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers February in Marbella offers a surprisingly pleasant window for a yacht charter — fewer crowds, lower rates, and generally stable weather. While the water is cooler than summer, daytime temperatures average 16°C with around 6 hours of sunshine, and the sea is often calm with winds of 8–10 knots. This guide covers what to expect, which boats suit the season, and how to make the most of off-peak deals. February Weather in Marbella: What to Expect February is mid-winter on the Costa del Sol, but the microclimate keeps things mild. Average highs reach 16°C, lows around 9°C. Sea temperature sits at 14–15°C — brisk but swimmable for the hardy. Rainfall averages 60 mm over 6–7 days, usually as short showers rather than all-day drizzle. Wind is typically from the west at 8–10 knots, producing a light chop but rarely cancelling charters. Daylight runs from approximately 08:15 to 18:45, giving you 10.5 hours for a full-day trip. Best Boats for a February Charter Enclosed or partially covered boats are ideal. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6 m, 12 pax) has a heated saloon and flybridge windscreen. The Ferretti 94 (29 m, 12 pax) offers luxurious indoor-outdoor flow. For smaller groups, the Astondoa 40 (12.5 m, 9 pax) and Azimut 39 (12.5 m, 11 pax) both have cabins with heating. If you prefer an open boat for sun, the Mangusta 80 (24 m, 12 pax) is a head-turner with a free jet ski included. February Charter Deals and Pricing Off-peak means lower rates. Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39) are available from €749 for 2 hours or €2,299 for a full 8-hour day — skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and VAT included. The Mangusta 80 (white or grey) starts at €4,719 for a minimum 4-hour charter, with a jet ski thrown in free. For other boats, including the Ferretti 94 and Canados 86 , pricing is on request via WhatsApp. All charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, soft drinks, beer, cava, white wine, light snacks, insurance, and Spanish IVA. Suggested Itineraries for February Short trips work well. From Puerto Banús, head east to Cabopino (20 minutes) for a sheltered anchorage and beach bar. West to Cala del Faro (15 minutes) offers dramatic cliffs and a quiet cove. For a longer cruise, continue to Estepona (40 minutes) or Sotogrande (1 hour) — both have marinas with winter dining options. Sunset trips are especially rewarding: the low sun casts gold over La Concha mountain, and a 2-hour charter on the Speedboat or Bandido costs from €749 (Tier A equivalent). What to Pack for a February Yacht Charter Layers are essential. Start with a long-sleeved base layer, add a fleece or light sweater, and top with a windproof jacket. Non-slip deck shoes or trainers are best. Sunglasses and sunscreen (SPF 30+) are still needed — the sun reflects off the water. A hat and gloves might be welcome during cruising, but once you anchor in a sheltered cove, the temperature rises quickly. The boat will have a cooler with drinks and snacks, but you can bring your own food. Wildlife and Sightseeing in February February is a good month for whale and dolphin watching. Common dolphins, pilot whales, and occasionally fin whales pass through the Strait of Gibraltar and can be seen off Marbella. Your skipper can adjust the route if conditions allow. Birdlife is abundant too — look for cormorants, gulls, and the occasional osprey along the cliffs near Cala Cortés. Frequently Asked Questions Is it warm enough for a yacht charter in Marbella in February? Yes, daytime highs average 16°C with plenty of sunshine. While swimming is bracing (sea temp 14–15°C), the sun on deck is pleasant. Bring a light jacket for cruising; the flybridge on boats like the Azimut 58 Flybridge offers shelter if needed. What is the sea state like off Marbella in February? Generally calm. The Mediterranean is sheltered by the coast, with typical wave heights under 0.5 m. Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter Weather in November: What to Know By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Thinking about a Marbella yacht charter in November ? You might be wondering if the weather holds up. The short answer: yes. November on the Costa del Sol offers mild air (15-20°C), calm seas, and fewer crowds – a surprisingly pleasant window for a relaxed cruise. This guide covers everything you need to know: sea temperature, wind, daylight, rain, and which boats suit the season. November Weather Overview for Marbella November is late autumn, but Marbella’s microclimate keeps it milder than inland Spain. Average highs reach 20°C, lows drop to 13°C. The sea temperature sits around 18°C – cool but not freezing. You’ll get roughly 10 hours of daylight, with sunset near 18:00. Rain is possible (5-7 wet days on average), but showers are usually brief and pass quickly. The Sierra Blanca mountains shield the coast from strong winds, so the sea stays flat for most of the month. Sea Temperature & Swimming Conditions The Mediterranean off Marbella averages 18°C in November . That’s a few degrees cooler than September (23°C) and October (21°C). For swimming, most people find it too chilly without a wetsuit. A 3/2mm short-sleeve wetsuit makes it comfortable. If you’re booking a Marbella yacht charter primarily for water sports, consider a boat that includes a jet ski – like the Mangusta 80 (€4,719 for 4 hours, jet ski included free) – because the adrenaline keeps you warm. Wind & Sea State November is one of the calmest months for wind along the Costa del Sol. Average wind speeds are 5-15 knots, often lighter in the morning. The famous Levante (easterly) wind is rare in autumn. Swell heights typically stay below 0.5-1 metre. That means smooth cruising for all our boats, from the Dubhe (8m, 5 pax) up to the Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax). If you’re prone to seasickness, November is a safe bet. Daylight Hours & Best Charter Times Sunrise: ~07:45. Sunset: ~18:00. That’s about 10 hours of usable daylight – enough for a half-day (4-5 hours) or full-day (8 hours) charter. For a half-day, a 10:00 or 11:00 start gives you the warmest part of the day. Full-day charters depart around 09:30 and return by 17:30, catching the golden hour. Popular November itineraries include a cruise from Puerto Banús to Cala del Faro (30 min) or a longer run to Estepona (45 min) for lunch ashore. Rain & Cloud Cover November averages 5-7 rainy days, but total precipitation is only about 60-80mm. Showers are typically short (15-30 minutes) and often happen overnight or early morning. Cloud cover varies: some days are bright with scattered clouds, others are completely clear. If you’re flexible, you can usually dodge rain by shifting your departure by an hour. All our boats have enclosed saloons with heating, so even a passing shower won’t ruin the trip. Best Boats for November Charters For cooler weather, boats with covered flybridges or spacious indoor saloons are ideal. Our top picks: Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) – heated lower helm, enclosed flybridge, stable ride. Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) – huge saloon, jet ski included, departs Puerto Banús. Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) – luxury interior, full climate control, perfect for entertaining. Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) – affordable option with a cosy cabin, €749/2h. All our charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, insurance, soft drinks, beer, wine, cava, light snacks, and 21% IVA. You can book online or message us on WhatsApp for custom quotes. What to Pack for a November Yacht Charter Layers are your friend. Start with a long-sleeve shirt or light sweater, add a fleece or windbreaker, and bring a waterproof shell just in case. Non-slip deck shoes (or trainers with good grip) are essential. Sunglasses and sunscreen are still necessary – UV index is moderate but reflection off the water adds exposure. A hat and a small towel are handy. If you plan to use the jet ski, a wetsuit is recommended (we can advise on local rental shops). Frequently Asked Questions Can you charter a yacht in Marbella in November? Yes. November is part of the low season, but most boats in our fleet operate year-round. We depart from Puerto Banús daily, weather permitting. The sea is calm, and you’ll enjoy more privacy and often lower rates than summer. What is the sea temperature in Marbella in November? The Mediterranean off Marbella averages 18°C in November. That’s too cool for casual swimming without a wetsuit, but fine for paddling or jet skiing. If you want to swim, a short-sleeve wetsuit (3/2mm) keeps you comfortable. Is November windy in Marbella? Not particularly. Average wind speeds are 5-15 knots, often lighter in the morning. The Costa del Sol is sheltered by the Sierra Blanca mountains, so gusts are rare. It’s a good month for a smooth, stable ride. What should I wear for a November yacht charter in Marbella? Layers. A long-sleeve top + light jacket or fleece works for the morning breeze. By midday (18-20°C), you may shed layers. Closed-toe non-slip shoes are smart. Sunglasses and sunscreen are still essential – UV is strong even in autumn. How many hours of daylight do you get in November? Sunrise is around 07:45 and sunset about 18:00, giving you roughly 10 hours of daylight. For a half-day charter (4-5 hours), aim for a 10:00 or 11:00 departure to enjoy the best light. Full-day charters (8 hours) work well if you start by 09:30. Do you offer any boats with heating or enclosed cabins? All our boats have enclosed saloons with heating and air conditioning. TITLE: What to Bring on a Marbella Yacht Charter 2038: Complete Checklist
DESCRIPTION: Complete checklist of what to bring on a yacht charter Marbella 2038: essentials, sun protection, swimwear, tech, and port fees. Plan your 8-hour trip from Puerto Banús.

Home › Guide › What to Bring on a Marbella Yacht Charter 2038: Complete Checklist By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Packing for a yacht charter in Marbella in 2038 is simpler than you think — but a few essentials make the difference between a good day and a great one. Whether you’re cruising from Puerto Banús to Cabopino , Estepona , or Sotogrande , this checklist covers clothing, sun protection, tech, and hidden costs like port fees. All our charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, insurance, and 21% VAT — so you can leave the logistics to us. Here’s what to bring for a seamless 8-hour trip on the Costa del Sol. Clothing and Footwear Light, breathable fabrics win on the Mediterranean. In July and August, air temps hit 33°C and sea temps 24°C. Pack a swimsuit, a cover-up, and a light jacket or windbreaker for the evening return — the breeze picks up after 6pm. No hard-soled shoes on deck: barefoot or non-marking soles only. Flip-flops are fine; boat shoes are better. If you plan to walk to a beach club (e.g., Nikki Beach or Ocean Club), bring trainers for the short stroll from the anchorage. Sun Protection and Health Essentials Marbella’s sun is intense — UV index 9+ from May to September. Bring reef-safe sun cream (oxybenzone-free) to comply with Marbella’s 2030 eco-bylaw; brands like Riemann P20 SPF50 or Green People SPF30 are sold at supermarkets in Puerto Banús. A wide-brimmed hat, polarised sunglasses, and a lip balm with SPF are non-negotiable. If you’re prone to motion sickness, take Kwells or Stugeron 30 minutes before departure — the Mediterranean is calm, but a few guests feel the swell near Cabo Pino. Tech and Entertainment Most boats have Bluetooth stereos, but a portable speaker in a dry bag is fine. Charge your phone and camera beforehand — power outlets are limited on smaller boats like the Dubhe (8m, 5 pax) or Mariah SX21. A waterproof phone pouch (€10 at Decathlon) lets you take photos from the swim platform. Download offline maps of Marbella and Estepona; mobile signal is strong near the coast but patchy in coves like Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés. Documents and Payments Every passenger needs a passport or national ID card. The crew may request a copy 24h before departure for the manifest. No visa needed for EU citizens; non-EU guests should check Spanish entry rules. Bring a card or cash for port fees — these are not included in the charter price. Typical costs: Cabopino €50–€80, Estepona €80–€120, Sotogrande €100–€150. If you want to stop at a beach club, budget €20–€50 per person for loungers and drinks. Food and Drink Every charter includes water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks (crisps, olives, nuts). For longer trips, pre-order a catering platter via WhatsApp: €25–€45 per person for cold meats, cheese, fruit, or sushi. No outside food is allowed unless pre-arranged — the crew needs to manage waste and allergens. If you have dietary restrictions, tell us 48h in advance and we’ll adjust the snacks. What NOT to Bring Hard luggage: No suitcases or large bags — space is limited. Use a soft duffel or backpack. Glass bottles: Most charters ban glass on deck for safety. Use plastic or metal reusable bottles. Illegal substances: Spanish law is strict. The crew will terminate the charter and call the port police if drugs are found. Pets: Only allowed on private charters with prior approval. Check via WhatsApp before booking. Seasonal Adjustments for 2038 Summer 2038 is expected to be hotter than average, with July highs of 35°C. Pack extra water (though we provide it). Spring and autumn trips (April–May, September–October) need a light jumper or fleece — sea breezes drop to 18°C after sunset. Winter charters (November–March) are rare but possible on the Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) ; bring a waterproof jacket and warm layers. No. Hard-soled shoes damage the teak deck. Barefoot or non-marking soles (e.g., boat shoes) are required. Most guests go barefoot or wear flip-flops. Bring a pair of trainers if you plan to walk to a beach club from the anchorage. Is sun cream provided on the charter? No. You must bring your own sun cream, and it must be reef-safe (oxybenzone-free) to comply with Marbella’s 2030 environmental bylaw. Brands like Riemann P20 or Green People SPF30 are widely available at supermarkets in Puerto Banús. What documents do I need to board? A valid passport or national ID card for every passenger. Crew may request a copy 24h before departure for the passenger manifest. No visa is required for EU citizens; non-EU guests should check Spanish entry rules. Will I get seasick on a Marbella yacht charter? Seasickness is rare in the Mediterranean due to calm seas, especially in summer. If you’re prone, take motion-sickness tablets (e.g., Kwells) 30 min before departure. The skipper will adjust speed if anyone feels queasy. Can I bring my own music speaker? Yes, but most boats have a Bluetooth stereo. If you bring a portable speaker, keep it in a dry bag. Avoid loud music near other anchored boats; the skipper may ask you to lower volume if it disturbs neighbours. What happens if it rains on the day of my charter? Marbella averages 320 sunny days per year, so rain is unlikely. If heavy rain is forecast, you can reschedule 48h before departure at no cost. Light drizzle doesn’t cancel trips; the flybridge or cabin provides cover. Are port fees included in the charter price? No. Port fees at stops like Cabopino (€50–€80), Estepona (€80–€120), or Sotogrande (€100–€150) are extra unless you pre-book an all-inclusive package. TITLE: What Food to Bring on a Marbella Yacht Charter: A Complete Guide
DESCRIPTION: Wondering what food to bring on a yacht charter Marbella? Our guide covers easy onboard meals, local Spanish dishes, and what the skipper provides. Start planning your menu now.

Home › Guide › What Food to Bring on a Marbella Yacht Charter: A Complete Guide By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning what food to bring on a yacht charter Marbella is one of the easiest ways to upgrade your day at sea. The good news? You don’t need to bring much – every charter already includes drinks and light snacks. But if you want a proper lunch, a celebration spread, or just better nibbles than crisps, a little planning goes a long way. This guide covers what’s provided, what to bring, and what to avoid, so you can focus on the views of La Concha and the Costa del Sol. What’s Already Included Every boat in our fleet – from the Astondoa 40 (€749/2h) to the Mangusta 80 (€4,719/4h) – comes with a licensed skipper, fuel, insurance, and a drinks-and-snacks package as standard. You’ll find water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks (olives, nuts, crisps) on board. That covers the first couple of hours comfortably. For longer trips, most guests bring extra food to share. Why Finger Foods Work Best at Sea Boats move. Even at anchor off Cala del Faro, there’s a gentle swell. Plates slide, glasses tip. The best foods are ones you can eat with your hands or a fork – no complicated plating. Think Spanish tortilla slices, jamón ibérico, manchego cheese, pan con tomate, prawns, and pre-cut melon. These are classic Marbella flavours and they travel well. What to Avoid on a Yacht Charter Skip anything that needs reheating (no oven on board), messy sauces, or fragile items like layered cakes with soft cream. Glass bottles are a safety hazard – transfer wine or oil into plastic containers. Also avoid anything that spoils quickly in the sun, like mayonnaise-based salads or raw shellfish. Stick to cured, dried, or fresh fruit options. Where to Buy Food Before You Board Puerto Banús has a Carrefour Express and several delis within a 3-minute walk of the dock. Marbella Marina has a Mercadona about 10 minutes on foot. For something special, try the jamón shops on the Golden Mile or the fish market in Estepona. Pre-order a platter from a local caterer and collect it on your way to the boat – most will pack it in a cool bag for you. Suggested Menu for a Full-Day Charter (8 Hours) Starter: Olives, almonds, jamón ibérico, manchego, and crusty bread Main: Spanish tortilla (cold), pan con tomate, grilled prawns (pre-cooked), mixed salad Dessert: Fresh fruit salad or a simple sponge cake Drinks: The cava and white wine already on board, plus your own soft drinks if you prefer This menu works for a group of 10-12 and needs zero cooking. Everything can be prepared the night before and stored in a cool bag until you board. Special Diets and Allergies The included snacks are generally vegetarian-friendly (olives, nuts, crisps). For gluten-free, vegan, or halal needs, bring your own food. The supermarkets in Puerto Banús stock a decent range of gluten-free bread and vegan cheese. If you’re booking a luxury yacht charter in Marbella , let the team know about allergies when you reserve – they can advise on local suppliers. What to Bring for Kids Children love simple, familiar food. Pack pre-cut fruit (watermelon, grapes), sandwiches, yoghurt tubes, and individual snacks. Avoid anything that crumbles (biscuits, dry cake) because the deck gets messy fast. Bring spill-proof cups for drinks – the provided cans are fine, but a reusable cup with a lid is better for little ones. Celebrations: Cakes, Prosecco, and Special Touches Birthday cakes, anniversary treats, and even small wedding cakes are welcome. Keep them in a sturdy box and ask the skipper to store in the fridge. Avoid cream-filled or soft-frosted cakes that melt in the heat. A simple lemon drizzle, fruit tart, or tray of pastries works perfectly. You can bring your own prosecco or champagne – just no glass bottles on deck. How Much to Bring for a Half-Day vs Full-Day For a 4-hour charter (€749 for the Astondoa 40), the included snacks plus a baguette, cheese, fruit, and a couple of drinks per person is plenty. For an 8-hour charter, plan a proper lunch – tortilla, cold meats, salad, and dessert. Most guests over-cater. A good rule: one substantial item (sandwich or tortilla slice) plus two snacks per person per hour. Frequently Asked Questions What food is already included on a Marbella yacht charter? Every charter with us includes a selection of water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks. The exact snacks vary but typically include crisps, nuts, olives, and sometimes crackers. This covers basic nibbles for the first hour or two. You are welcome to bring additional food to supplement. Can I bring my own food on board? Yes, absolutely. You can bring any food you like. Most guests bring their own lunch, picnic, or celebration cake. Just avoid glass bottles (use plastic or cans) and remember the galley is basic – no oven, just a fridge and sometimes a hob. Finger foods and pre-prepared items work best. What are the best foods to bring on a yacht charter in Marbella? Stick to easy, no-fuss options: Spanish jamón ibérico, manchego cheese, tortilla española, pan con tomate, olives, prawns, pre-cut fruit, and crusty bread. These taste great at sea, don’t need reheating, and pair well with the cava and white wine already on board. Is there a fridge on the boat? Yes, all our boats have a small fridge or cool box. It’s enough to keep drinks cold and store pre-cut fruit, cheese, and cured meats. But don’t expect a full kitchen or freezer. If you bring anything that needs to stay cold, pack it in a cool bag and transfer to the fridge on arrival. Can I order catering or have food delivered to the boat? Yes. Many guests order from local delis in Puerto Banús or Marbella Marina. You can collect it yourself or arrange delivery to the dock. Popular options include platters of jamón, cheese, seafood, and sushi. Just give the caterer your departure time so it’s ready when you arrive. What about special dietary requirements? The included snacks are generally vegetarian-friendly (olives, nuts, crisps). If you have specific allergies or preferences, bring your own food. The skipper can advise on nearby shops. For gluten-free or vegan options, the supermarkets in Puerto Banús stock a good range. How much food should I bring for a half-day charter? For a 4-hour charter, a light lunch or substantial snacks are enough. Think a baguette, some cheese, cured meat, fruit, and maybe a small dessert. For an 8-hour full-day charter, plan a proper lunch – tortilla, salad, cold meats, and plenty of fruit. Most guests over-cater, so start smaller. Can I bring a birthday cake or celebration food? Yes, cakes and celebration treats are welcome. Just keep them in a sturdy box and ask the skipper to store it in the fridge. Avoid anything with cream that might spoil in the heat. A simple sponge, fruit tart, or tray of pastries works well. Planning what food to bring on a yacht charter Marbella is simple once you know the basics. Pack light, think Spanish flavours, and leave the cooking to the skipper. Ready to book? Home › Guide › What to Eat on a Yacht Charter in Marbella: Onboard Menu Ideas By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Deciding what to eat on a yacht charter in Marbella can make the difference between a good day and a great one. With the Mediterranean sun, the Costa del Sol coastline, and a chilled glass of cava in hand, the right food elevates the whole experience. Every charter from Boat Rental Marbella includes light snacks, soft drinks, beer, cava, and white wine. But you are free to bring your own food or arrange catering. This guide covers menu ideas, local catering tips, and practical advice for eating well on the water. What's Already Included in Your Charter Before you plan extra food, know what comes standard. All our charters include water, soft drinks (Coca-Cola, Sprite, tonic), beer (local lager), white wine, cava, and light snacks such as nuts, olives, crisps, or similar. The skipper will have these ready when you board. If you have specific preferences or dietary needs, tell us at booking and we can adjust the included snacks. For a 2-hour Tier A charter (Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 at €749), this is usually enough. For longer trips, you may want to bring more substantial food. Best Foods to Bring Onboard Think portable, easy to eat, and not too heavy. The Mediterranean heat means food that sits well without refrigeration for a while is ideal. Here are top choices: Fresh seafood: Grilled prawns, lobster salad, or cold seafood platter. Many Marbella supermarkets sell ready-to-eat seafood. Spanish tapas: Jamón ibérico, manchego cheese, chorizo, patatas bravas, tortilla española, and pimientos de padrón. Easy to share and full of flavour. Paella: A classic. Order a takeaway paella from a local restaurant and reheat in the galley. Works best on a full-day charter. Salads: Greek salad, Niçoise, or a simple tomato and mozzarella. Light and refreshing. Fruit: Grapes, melon, berries, or sliced mango. Hydrating and sweet. Bread and dips: Baguette, olive oil, hummus, tzatziki. Simple and satisfying. Catering Options in Marbella If you prefer not to shop yourself, you can arrange catering delivered to your boat at Puerto Banús. Several Marbella-based providers specialise in yacht charters. Options range from gourmet picnic baskets (€25–€45 per person) to full multi-course meals (€60–€120 per person). Typical menus include ceviche, grilled octopus, beef tenderloin, and chocolate tart. We can share contact details via WhatsApp. Just let us know the number of guests and any dietary requirements. Catering is not included in the charter price, so budget accordingly. Drinks: What to Bring Yourself The included drinks cover the basics, but many guests bring their own champagne, rosé, or red wine. There is no corkage fee. You can also bring spirits, mixers, or speciality beers. The galley fridge has space for your bottles. For a full-day charter on a Mangusta 80 (€4,719 minimum 4h, includes a free jet ski), you might want a magnum of Champagne for a special celebration. Ice is provided. Remember to bring plenty of water—especially on hot summer days when temperatures reach 35°C. What to Avoid Eating on a Yacht Seasickness is the main concern. Avoid heavy, greasy foods like fried chicken, burgers, or creamy sauces. Spicy dishes can also upset the stomach. Messy foods like sticky ribs or saucy wings are hard to eat while the boat is moving. Strong-smelling foods (blue cheese, fish sauce) are best left ashore. Stick to light, fresh, and easy-to-manage items. If anyone in your group is prone to motion sickness, ginger biscuits or crackers are a good backup. Practical Tips for Eating on Board Most of our yachts have a galley with a fridge, sink, and storage. The larger yachts like the Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) or Canados 86 (26m, 12 pax) have full kitchens. The skipper can help with serving and basic food prep. Bring your own plates, cutlery, and glasses if you want something more elegant than the disposable ones provided. A cooler bag with ice packs keeps extra drinks cold. If you anchor for a swim stop, food stays fresh longer. For a full guide to onboard menus , check our dedicated post. Ricardo Soriano, Marbella) – gourmet food hall with high-quality products. All are within a 10-minute drive of Puerto Banús. Ask your skipper if you need directions. The included light snacks can be swapped for suitable alternatives. Caterers in Marbella are experienced with dietary restrictions. For example, a vegan menu might include vegetable paella, hummus, grilled vegetables, and fresh fruit. Always confirm at least 48 hours before departure. TITLE: What to Wear on a Yacht Charter in Marbella: Dress Code Guide
DESCRIPTION: Wondering what to wear on a yacht charter Marbella? Our dress code guide covers daytime chic, evening glam, and practical essentials for Puerto Banús.

Home › Guide › What to Wear on a Yacht Charter in Marbella: Dress Code Guide By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a day on the water in Marbella and wondering what to wear on a yacht charter Marbella style? Whether you're cruising from Puerto Banús to Cabopino or anchoring off the Golden Mile, dressing right makes the experience more comfortable and enjoyable. The Mediterranean sun, sea breezes, and elegant surroundings call for a balance of practicality and style. This guide covers everything from daytime essentials to evening attire, so you step aboard looking the part—without a wardrobe mishap. Daytime Dress Code: Casual Comfort For daytime charters—especially on boats like the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39—lightweight fabrics are your best friend. Linen shirts, cotton T-shirts, shorts, sundresses, and swimwear are all appropriate. Light colours reflect heat better than dark ones, so stick to whites, pastels, or neutrals. Bring a swimsuit underneath for spontaneous dips at Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés. A wide-brim hat and polarised sunglasses are essential for sun protection. The temperature on the water can feel 5°C cooler than on land, so a light cotton cardigan or linen jacket is handy. Evening & Sunset Cruises: Smart-Casual Glam Sunset charters in Marbella have a more polished vibe. For a 4-hour Mangusta 80 sunset cruise, men can wear chinos or tailored shorts with a button-down shirt; women often choose a summer dress, jumpsuit, or smart separates. Avoid ripped denim, gym wear, or overly casual flip-flops. A light jacket or pashmina is useful as temperatures drop after sunset. If you're dining at a Puerto Banús restaurant afterwards, smart-casual is the norm—think resort wear rather than black-tie. Footwear: Keep It Deck-Friendly The number one rule: no heels. Stilettos and dark soles damage teak decks. Stick to non-marking white-soled shoes such as boat shoes, espadrilles, or rubber-soled sandals. Barefoot is perfectly acceptable and often preferred on deck. Many charters, including our fleet departing from Puerto Banús, ask guests to remove shoes before stepping aboard. If you're unsure, bring a pair of clean, white-soled trainers or slip-ons. Sun Protection & Layering The Andalusian sun is strong even in spring and autumn. SPF 50+ sunscreen (reef-safe preferred), a wide-brim hat, and polarised sunglasses are non-negotiable. Sea breezes can make you feel cooler than you are, so you might not notice sunburn until later. Layering is key: a lightweight windbreaker or cashmere wrap fits easily in a tote and can be pulled on when the wind picks up or after sunset. Our Boat Rental Marbella team always recommends bringing a layer even on hot days. What to Wear for Swimming Stops Most charters include a swim stop at spots like Cabopino or Cala del Faro. Bring a swimsuit, a dry change of clothes, and a towel. Wet swimsuits on upholstery can cause damage, so use the fresh-water shower on board to rinse off and change into dry clothes. A quick-dry sarong or kaftan is perfect for covering up between swims. Our boats provide soft drinks, water, and snacks, so you don't need to bring your own cooler. Accessories & Practical Items A small crossbody bag or waterproof phone pouch keeps your phone, sunscreen, and sunglasses safe and hands-free. Leave large totes or backpacks in the cabin. Polarised sunglasses reduce glare from the sea and make navigation safer. A watch with a rubber strap (not leather) can handle splashes. Avoid heavy jewellery—saltwater and sun can damage delicate pieces. If you're prone to seasickness, bring acupressure bands or medication; the crew can store them safely. What Not to Wear Avoid dark colours that absorb heat, especially on sunny days. Skip heels, dark-soled shoes, and anything with rivets or studs that could scratch surfaces. Ripped denim, gym shorts, and offensive slogans are not appropriate for the upscale Marbella charter scene. Also avoid heavy perfumes or colognes—they can attract insects and become overpowering in the sun. Finally, don't wear your best silk or delicate fabrics; salt spray and sunscreen can stain them. Dressing for Different Yacht Types Your attire may vary slightly depending on the boat. On a sporty speedboat like the Mariah SX21 or Dubhe, swimwear and casual clothes are fine. On a luxury motor yacht like the Ferretti 94 or Mangusta 80, smart-casual is more appropriate. For a catamaran like the Lagoon 380, barefoot comfort and relaxed beachwear work well. Always check with your charter company if you're unsure—our team at Boat Rental Marbella is happy to advise. Packing Checklist for a Marbella Yacht Charter Lightweight linen or cotton clothing (shorts, shirts, dresses) Swimsuit and dry change of clothes Non-marking white-soled shoes or boat shoes Wide-brim hat and polarised sunglasses SPF 50+ sunscreen (reef-safe) Light jacket, cardigan, or pashmina Small crossbody bag or waterproof phone pouch Towel and quick-dry sarong or kaftan Final Tips Remember that Marbella's microclimate can shift quickly—a sunny morning might turn breezy by afternoon. Check the weather forecast before you depart. Most importantly, dress in layers so you can adapt. If you're celebrating a special occasion, feel free to dress up a notch; the crew will appreciate the effort. For more advice on planning your day, browse our Boat Rental Marbella guide or contact us directly on WhatsApp for personalised tips. Frequently Asked Questions Can I wear heels on a yacht charter in Marbella? No. High heels, especially stilettos, can damage teak decks and are not allowed on most charter yachts. Opt for flat sandals, espadrilles, or boat shoes with non-marking soles. If you need height for evening photos, choose block heels or wedges under 5 cm, but check with your skipper first. What should I wear for a daytime yacht charter in Marbella? For daytime, choose lightweight, breathable fabrics like linen, cotton, or jersey. A white shirt, linen shorts, or a sundress works well. Bring a swimsuit underneath for swimming stops at Cala del Faro or Cabopino. A wide-brim hat, polarised sunglasses, and SPF 50+ sunscreen are non-negotiable. Is there a dress code for evening yacht charters in Puerto Banús? Yes. Sunset and evening charters in Puerto Banús lean towards smart-casual or cocktail attire. Men can wear chinos or tailored shorts with a button-down shirt; women often choose a summer dress or jumpsuit. Avoid overly casual items like ripped denim or gym wear. A light jacket or pashmina is useful as temperatures drop. What shoes are safe for a yacht deck? Non-marking white-soled shoes are the safest choice. Boat shoes, espadrilles, or rubber-soled sandals work well. Barefoot is also common and comfortable on deck. Avoid black or dark soles that can leave scuff marks. The skipper may ask you to remove shoes if they mark the teak. Do I need to bring a change of clothes for swimming? Yes. Bring a dry change of clothes and a towel if you plan to swim. Wet swimsuits on upholstery can cause damage and discomfort. Most yachts have a fresh-water shower to rinse off after a dip. Store wet items in a waterproof bag provided by the crew. What accessories are essential for a Marbella yacht charter? Polarised sunglasses reduce glare from the sea. A wide-brim hat protects your face and neck. A crossbody bag or waterproof phone pouch keeps your phone and valuables secure. Reef-safe sunscreen (SPF 50+) is a must. A light scarf or windbreaker is handy for afternoon breezes. Can I wear jewellery on a yacht charter? Yes, but keep it minimal. Saltwater and sun can damage delicate metals and gemstones. Leave valuable heirloom pieces at home. Simple gold or silver earrings, a watch, and a necklace are fine. Avoid long chains or dangling bracelets that could catch on rigging or deck fittings. What should I not wear on a yacht charter in Marbella? Avoid dark or black clothing on sunny days

First Impressions: Design and Build The Pershing 46 cuts a sharp profile. With its low-slung hull, aggressive bow, and sleek flybridge, it looks fast even at the dock. The build quality is unmistakably Italian—every surface, from the teak decking to the stainless steel fittings, feels premium. The cockpit is spacious for a boat of this size, with a large sunbed aft and a dinette area that converts into an additional lounging space. The flybridge is compact but well laid out, with a helm station that puts you in control and a small seating area for guests who want to ride up top. Onboard Comfort: Cabins, Saloon, and Galley Below deck, the Pershing 46 sleeps up to four guests in two cabins. The master cabin is forward with a double berth, while the VIP cabin has twin single beds. Both are finished in light wood and cream upholstery, creating a bright, airy feel. The saloon is compact but functional, with a small galley equipped with a sink, fridge, and hob—enough for preparing snacks and drinks during the day. The head is clean and well-sized, with a separate shower. While you wouldn’t live aboard for a week, it’s perfectly comfortable for day charters, offering a cool retreat from the Mediterranean sun. Performance and Handling The Pershing 46 is powered by twin diesel engines that push it to a top speed of around 38 knots. Cruising at 30–35 knots is smooth and stable, with minimal vibration even in moderate chop. The hull is deep-V, which cuts through the sea cleanly, and the steering is responsive—exactly what you’d expect from a brand that builds performance boats. For a day charter, this means you can cover more ground: from Puerto Banús to Estepona in under 20 minutes, or zip out to Cabopino for a swim stop and still have time for a leisurely lunch. Fairline Targa 12m overview The Fairline Targa 12m is a British-built 12-metre sport cruiser with a slightly more relaxed character than the Pershing. It also carries up to 10 passengers and reaches a top speed of about 32 knots. The cockpit layout is similar, with a large sunpad and a sociable seating area, but the Fairline's design emphasises comfort and practicality. The cabin includes a double berth and a separate WC. Specs at a glance Feature Pershing 46 Sunseeker Predator 55 Length 14m 17.4m Beam 4.2m 4.6m Passengers 10 10 Engines 2× 800hp 2× 800hp (typical) Top speed ~45 knots ~35 knots Departure Puerto Banús Puerto Banús (not in our fleet) We don't currently offer the Sunseeker Predator 55, but we do offer the Pershing 46 and the similar-sized Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) for comparison. Performance: speed vs comfort The Pershing 46 is a thoroughbred. With twin 800hp engines, it planes quickly and cruises at 40 knots without breaking a sweat. You'll feel the acceleration in your stomach as you leave Puerto Banús behind. The Sunseeker Predator 55 is no slouch, but it's built more for cruising comfort. Its deeper V-hull and heavier displacement mean a softer ride in choppy seas, but you'll trade top-end speed. For a 2-hour blast to Cala del Faro and back, the Pershing 46 is the clear winner. For a full-day cruise down to Estepona or Sotogrande, the Predator's stability pays off. Deck layout and onboard experience The Pershing 46 has a sporty, low-profile cockpit with a large sunpad aft and a well-protected helm. It's intimate — perfect for 4–6 people. The Predator 55, being 3.4m longer, offers a more expansive cockpit with a wet bar, L-shaped seating, and a bigger swim platform. You can spread out more, and the higher freeboard gives a sense of security. If you're planning to anchor and swim, the Pershing 46's swim platform is closer to the water, making entry easier. TITLE: Private vs Shared Boat Charter in Marbella: Cost &amp; Experience
DESCRIPTION: Private vs shared boat charter in Marbella: full cost-per-head math, who fits each model, the trade-offs in itinerary control, anchor time and atmosphere.

Home › Guide › Private vs Shared Boat Charter in Marbella: Cost & Experience By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Marbella's charter market splits into two models: private (you book the whole boat and a skipper, set your own itinerary) and shared (you buy seats on a fixed-time, fixed-route trip with 8-30 strangers). Both work. The right choice depends on group size, budget and how much itinerary control matters to you. The cost-per-head math Group size Private 2h (€275 total) Shared 2h (€60/head) Winner 2 €137/head €60/head Shared 4 €69/head €60/head Shared, marginal 6 €46/head €60/head Private 8 €34/head €60/head Private 10 €28/head €60/head Private Break-even sits between 4 and 5 guests. Below that, shared is genuinely cheaper. Above 6, private beats shared on cost and delivers the full private experience. For broader pricing context see how much it costs to rent a boat in Marbella . Beyond cost Itinerary control Private: total control. Want to skip the sunset anchor and go further west? Want to swim for 90 minutes at Cala del Faro? Done. Want to head to a specific beach club for a tender-in lunch? Done. The skipper executes your day. Shared: none. The boat leaves at a fixed time, follows a fixed route (usually Puerto Banús-Río Verde-back), anchors for a fixed swim, returns at a fixed time. Skipper-led. Group fit Private: any group from 2 to 40 depending on boat. Pure family time, pure stag time, pure romantic time — the deck is yours. Best for stag/hen weekends (see boat party Marbella ), couples wanting privacy, families with kids, corporate groups. Shared: couples or pairs who don't mind mingling, solo travellers, friend groups of 2-3 who want a social vibe. Works less well for groups of 4+ who'd rather be on their own. Anchor time and swim Private: typically 60-90 minutes of anchor time on a 2-hour trip, 2-3 hours on a 4-hour trip. You set the pace. Shared: 30-45 minutes max anchor time on a 2-hour shared trip, often with 20-30 people competing for the swim ladder. Suits people happy with a brief dip. Vibe Private: exactly the energy your group brings. Quiet family swim, raucous birthday, romantic sunset — your choice. Shared: a friendly Mediterranean mixer most days. Sometimes lively (a few cava drinks in, music up); occasionally awkward if the group composition is off. Skipper sets the tone. What's typically shared in Marbella Sunset 2-hour cruises from Puerto Banús or Marbella Marina — €45-€65 per seat, departing 60-75 min before sunset. Dolphin-watching half-days from Sotogrande or Puerto Banús — €35-€55 per seat for 2-3 hours including the offshore run. Sailing day trips — €70-€110 per seat for 4-5 hours on a 50+ ft sailing yacht with 10-12 guests. Party catamaran day trips — €60-€90 per seat, mixed crowd, music, beach club stops included. What's almost always private in Marbella Full-day yacht charters (8 hours) — see yacht charter Marbella Fishing charters — see fishing boat rental Marbella Luxury and superyacht charters — see luxury yacht rental Marbella Licence-free self-drive — see no-license boats Anything for stag/hen groups Gibraltar day trips — see Gibraltar by boat The right call Choose shared if: you're 2 people, you only want 2 hours on the water, you don't mind a fixed route, and you like the idea of meeting other guests. Choose private if: you're 4+ people, you want any kind of itinerary control, you're celebrating something, you're chartering for a full day, or you simply prefer your own group. Most charters in Marbella sold to visitors are private — for good reason. Compare actual private boats on the boat rental Marbella hub, or message us with your group size for tailored options. Frequently asked questions Is shared cheaper than private? Only for small groups. Break-even is around 4 guests. Above 6 guests, private is cheaper per head AND delivers the full private experience. Can I customise a shared trip? No. Shared charters run fixed route, fixed times, fixed anchor stops. You need a private charter for any customisation. What's the atmosphere like on a shared boat? Mixed — 8-30 strangers for 2-4 hours. Often friendly, occasionally awkward. Private charters give total control over the vibe. Can I find a shared charter on the day? Sometimes — sunset and dolphin tours run daily in summer with seats often available 24-48 hours ahead. Saturday peak slots fill in advance. Last-minute private charters are also available on weekdays in shoulder season but harder in peak July-August. Is the safety standard different? No — both formats use commercially-licensed boats, qualified skippers, and the same Spanish maritime regulations (life jackets, safety briefings, weather rules). The difference is in itinerary control and group size, not safety. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

TITLE: How to Propose on a Yacht in Marbella: Captain&#x27;s Playbook
DESCRIPTION: Plan a yacht proposal in Marbella: 7 captain-tested moments, sunset timing, ring safety and pricing from €749/2h. Book your moment now.

Home › Guide › How to Propose on a Yacht in Marbella: Captain's Playbook By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers A yacht proposal in Marbella works because three things line up: glass-flat water in the late-afternoon lee of La Concha, a skipper who knows when to disappear, and a 60-minute golden hour that flatters every photograph. After years of running these charters out of Puerto Banús, the playbook below is how we set it up — from ring handover on the dock to the champagne pour at anchor. Browse our full fleet on the boat rental hub when you've finished reading. The three questions to settle first Before you pick a yacht, lock down: (1) couples-only or family-and-friends, (2) sunset or daylight, and (3) anchored moment or moving moment. Couples-only on a Tier A yacht keeps the cost under €1,500 all-in and the energy intimate. Family-and-friends pushes you to the Mangusta 80 or Canados 86 and turns the day into a celebration with the proposal as the opening act. Most clients we host go couples-only; the post-yes party then happens ashore at a Puerto Banús restaurant. Picking the right boat from our fleet The Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39 are the proposal workhorses: 12.5 m, nine passengers, a forward sunpad that doubles as the staging area, and a price tag of €749 for two hours. For a more theatrical setting we use the Mangusta 80 — 24 m of polished teak, a 12-person capacity for the family option, and a swim platform you can light with floating LEDs. Sportier engagements suit the Pershing 46 or Fairline Targa 12m. Avoid the Lagoon 380 catamaran for proposals: stable, but the layout makes the moment feel like a group activity even when it isn't. Boat Length Pax Best for From Astondoa 40 12.5 m 9 Intimate couples charter €749 / 2h Azimut 39 12.5 m 11 Couples + small party €749 / 2h Pershing 46 14 m 10 Sporty proposal run On request Mangusta 80 24 m 12 Family-and-friends celebration €4,719 / 4h Canados 86 26 m 12 Statement charter On request Timing the sunset to the minute Marbella's sunset shifts roughly 75 minutes between May and August. In June and July the sun touches the horizon at 21:35, so the proposal moment lands at 20:55-21:05; board at 19:30, leave the marina by 19:45. In May and September aim for boarding at 18:00 and the question at 19:30. Sunset proposals in October work too, but you'll lose the swim option as the water drops below 20 °C. We never recommend a noon proposal — the light is brutal, the deck is hot, and the wind is usually 4-6 kts stronger than the evening lull. The route: where to drop the hook From Puerto Banús we run westbound along the Golden Mile, past the Marbella Club, and anchor in one of two spots: Río Verde at 2.5 NM out with La Concha mountain framed dead-astern, or Cala Cortés at 4 NM with the more dramatic cliff backdrop. Río Verde has shallower 6-8 m water and a faster recovery to the marina; Cala Cortés gives you privacy from passing day-trippers and a clean western horizon for the ring shot. For a longer charter on the Mangusta 80 we add a Cala del Faro stop and return via Estepona's lee shore. Read more about the local cruising grounds on our Puerto Banús guide . Ring safety: the captain's protocol This is the part couples underestimate. Salt water, polished teak and a 4 kt wind eat ring boxes for breakfast. Our protocol: you hand the box to the captain on the dock before your partner walks down the pontoon. It goes into a zipped wheelhouse drawer for the outbound leg. When you give the agreed signal — usually a request for "another glass of cava" — the skipper retrieves the box and slips it to you under a folded linen napkin during the drinks service. Open the box only when seated, on a non-slip cushion, with both hands. Never on the bow underway, never on the swim platform, never near an open guardrail. Photography and video without it feeling staged The photographer boards 10 minutes before you, in beachwear, and shoots from the flybridge with a 70-200 mm lens. From that elevated angle they capture the moment without appearing in the frame or breaking the intimacy. We do not recommend a drone for the proposal moment itself — the noise telegraphs the surprise — but a drone shot of the anchored yacht 5 minutes after the yes is spectacular. Allow €350-500 for a Marbella-based wedding photographer; we can recommend three we work with regularly. Forward their contact via WhatsApp when you book. The drinks, the flowers, the playlist Every charter includes cava, white wine, beer, soft drinks and light snacks at no extra cost. For a proposal we upgrade the cava pour to a chilled bottle held back specifically for the moment — tell us at booking. A florist arrangement (single-stem roses to a full bow garland) runs €120-180 via the Puerto Banús flower market; we collect it the morning of. Bring your own playlist on a phone — the saloon has Bluetooth, but pre-test it. The captain's default is silence at the anchor moment, which works better than any song. See our on-board catering options if you want canapés or a full dinner course added. What it costs, end to end A typical sunset proposal on the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39: €749 charter (2 hours, all drinks and IVA included), €150 florist, €450 photographer, €0 captain tip expectation but €50-80 is standard if the service deserves it. Total: €1,349-1,429. Scaling up to the Mangusta 80 with eight family members aboard: €4,719 charter (4 hours), €300 florist, €600 photographer, €1,200 added catering = roughly €6,800. Everything in the Tier A package is priced transparently on our pricing page ; the Tier B and bespoke quotes come back within an hour on WhatsApp. Booking the date Saturday sunsets in July and August fill 6-8 weeks ahead. May, June and September sit at 3-4 weeks. Midweek is easier and gives the captain more flexibility on departure time. If you're inside the 72-hour window, message us directly — last-minute openings appear when other charters reschedule. Confirm the date, the boat, and one critical detail we always ask: does your partner already suspect a yacht day? If yes, we run a decoy itinerary (a swim stop at Cala Cortés) so the proposal moment still surprises. Frequently asked questions What is the best yacht for a proposal in Marbella? For couples-only privacy, the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 (Tier A, €749/2h) give you a 12.5 m deck, a sunpad bow and a discreet skipper — ideal for the moment itself. If you're folding the proposal into a celebration with family, the Mangusta 80 at €4,719/4h sleeps the party across 24 m of teak with a swim platform large enough for a photographer. What time should I plan the proposal? Aim to drop the question 25-30 minutes before sunset, with the bow pointed west toward Estepona. In June-July that means a 19:30 boarding and the moment around 20:55; in May or September shift everything 75 minutes earlier. Boarding earlier than this risks a hot, glaring deck; later means rushed photos in fading light. How do I get the ring on board without ruining the surprise? Hand the box to the captain on the dock before your partner boards — we lock it in the wheelhouse drawer. When you signal, the skipper retrieves it and slips it to you under a folded napkin during the drinks service. Never carry it in a swimsuit pocket or set it down on a moving deck; one wave and it's a 30-metre dive recovery. Can I bring a photographer or videographer? Yes — every charter includes the photographer in the 9-12 passenger count at no extra charge. We recommend boarding them 10 minutes before you and your partner so they can pre-position on the flybridge or bow. For the Mangusta 80 there's room for a second-angle operator on the swim platform; just confirm 48 hours ahead via WhatsApp. What if my partner gets seasick? The 18:00-21:00 window we use for proposals coincides with the daily wind drop — Poniente westerlies usually fall under 8 kts and swell sits below 0.5 m inside the M

URL: https://boatrentalinmarbella.com/blog/puerto-banus-vs-cabopino-departure/
TITLE: Puerto Banús vs Cabopino: Best Departure Port in Marbella
DESCRIPTION: Puerto Banús vs Cabopino departure: which Marbella port suits your boat charter? Compare distances, costs, and local vibes to pick the best start point.

Home › Guide › Puerto Banús vs Cabopino: Best Departure Port in Marbella By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Choosing between Puerto Banús and Cabopino as your departure port for a boat charter in Marbella can shape your entire day on the water. While both are beautiful spots on the Costa del Sol, they offer very different experiences – especially when it comes to convenience, atmosphere, and access to our fleet. In this guide, we break down the key differences so you can decide which port suits your trip best. Location and distance Puerto Banús sits at the heart of Marbella’s Golden Mile, just west of the town centre. Cabopino is roughly 5 nautical miles further west, near the border of Marbella and Estepona. By boat, it’s a 20-minute cruise at 15 knots – barely enough time to finish your first drink. On a faster vessel like the Pershing 46 (14m, 10 pax), you can cover the distance in under 10 minutes. Departure ports: what’s available All our boats depart from Puerto Banús – the Astondoa 40, Azimut 39, Mangusta 80 (white, grey, and original), Azimut 58 Flybridge, Canados 86, Ferretti 94, and every other vessel in our fleet. Cabopino has a smaller marina that primarily services local fishing boats and a handful of private yachts; there are no charter operators with the range of boats we offer. If you want to charter a premium motor yacht or a spacious catamaran like the Lagoon 380 (11.6m, 10 pax), Puerto Banús is your only choice. Parking and access Puerto Banús has several paid parking garages (€25–€40 per day) and limited street parking. It’s busy, especially in summer, but you’re steps away from restaurants, shops, and the dock. Cabopino offers free parking in a large lot near the marina, but spaces fill up quickly on weekends. If you’re driving from Marbella town, Puerto Banús is more convenient – it’s a 10-minute drive along the A-7, whereas Cabopino is 15–20 minutes further. Atmosphere and amenities Puerto Banús is synonymous with luxury: designer boutiques, world-class dining at Ocean Club and Pangea, and a buzzing nightlife scene. Cabopino is quieter, with a handful of chiringuitos like Chiringuito Cabopino, a small beach, and a relaxed vibe. If you’re after a lively start to your charter – grabbing a coffee or a glass of cava before boarding – Puerto Banús wins hands down. For a chilled, low-key departure, Cabopino has its charm, but you’ll need to arrange your own transport to the marina. Fuel and costs All our charters include fuel, skipper, insurance, and taxes. The price you see – for example, €749 for 2 hours on an Azimut 39 – covers everything from departure to return. The fuel cost from Puerto Banús to Cabopino and back is roughly €50–€100 depending on boat size and speed. On a Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax, €4,719 for 4 hours), the fuel burn is higher, but it’s all included in the price. You won’t pay extra for distance. Best boat for a Cabopino trip For a short trip to Cabopino, the Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax, €749/2h) is a fantastic value – enough space for a group of friends, a comfortable flybridge, and a decent speed. If you’re celebrating something special, the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) offers a flybridge, sun pads, and a jet ski included free. For a small group, the Speedboat (7m, 6 pax) or Mariah SX21 (6.4m, 5 pax) are nimble options for a quick blast over. Which port should you choose? If convenience, luxury, and access to a wide fleet matter most, Puerto Banús is the clear winner. You’ll have parking, restaurants, and our full range of boats – from the Dubhe (8m, 5 pax) to the Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax). Cabopino is a great destination to visit during your charter, but not a practical departure point. Book your charter from Puerto Banús and enjoy a short, scenic cruise to Cabopino for lunch or a swim. Frequently asked questions Can I charter a boat from Cabopino? No, we operate exclusively from Puerto Banús. All our boats depart from Puerto Banús Marina, including the Astondoa 40, Azimut 39, and Mangusta 80. Cabopino has a small marina but no charter operators with the range of boats we offer. How long does it take to sail from Puerto Banús to Cabopino? It takes approximately 20 minutes at 15 knots to cover the 5 NM distance. On a fast boat like the Pershing 46 (which cruises at 30+ knots), you can make it in under 10 minutes. Is Cabopino worth visiting on a day charter? Yes, Cabopino is a great stop for lunch at Chiringuito Cabopino or a swim at the nearby dunes beach. The marina is small but charming, and it’s a quiet alternative to the bustle of Puerto Banús. What are the parking options at Puerto Banús vs Cabopino? Puerto Banús has paid parking garages (approx. €25–€40 per day) and limited street parking. Cabopino offers free parking in a large lot near the marina, but it can fill up quickly in summer. Which port has better restaurants and nightlife? Puerto Banús is world-famous for its upscale restaurants, beach clubs, and nightlife – think Ocean Club, Pangea, and La Sala. Cabopino is quieter, with a handful of chiringuitos and a relaxed vibe. Are there any extra costs to depart from Puerto Banús? No, our charter prices include all port fees, fuel, skipper, and taxes. You pay the listed price – for example, €749 for 2 hours on an Azimut 39 – and that covers everything from departure to return. Can I swim or anchor near Cabopino? Yes, the waters off Cabopino are calm and clear, with a sandy bottom perfect for anchoring. The nearby dunes beach (Playa de Cabopino) is a popular spot for swimming and snorkelling. TITLE: Puerto Banús Yacht Fuel Surcharge Explained 2026
DESCRIPTION: Puerto Banús yacht fuel surcharge explained: why most charters add 20-40% in fuel, and how our all-inclusive €749 rate avoids it. Book on WhatsApp.

Home › Guide › Puerto Banús Yacht Fuel Surcharge Explained 2026 By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers The advertised price for a Puerto Banús yacht charter and the figure you actually pay at the pontoon are often two different numbers, and the gap is usually called a fuel surcharge . In 2026, with marine diesel hovering around €1.55-€1.85 per litre on the Costa del Sol, that surcharge can add 20-40% to a quoted rate. This guide explains how the surcharge is calculated, why it exists, and why our charters — bookable through the Boat Rental Marbella hub — do not use one. What a fuel surcharge actually is A fuel surcharge is a line item added after the base hire. It can be structured three ways: a flat percentage (commonly 25-35%) of the charter price, a per-litre charge based on the boat's actual consumption that day, or a fixed euro amount per engine hour. The first model is most common in Puerto Banús because it is simplest to quote over WhatsApp. The second is more honest but requires trust in the operator's logbook. The third sits somewhere in between. Either way, the surcharge is real money. A €1,200 half-day on a 14m flybridge can become €1,560-€1,680 once a 30-40% surcharge lands. Guests rarely see the breakdown until the final invoice. Why marine diesel costs more than road diesel Marine fuel in Spain is sold under different tax and logistics conditions than the diesel you put in a hire car. Pontoon delivery, lower turnover at marina pumps, and supplier margin on small-volume sales all push the price up. In June 2026 at Puerto Banús, expect €1.55-€1.85 per litre depending on supplier and whether you fuel from the pump or a bunker truck. Estepona and Sotogrande sit in the same band; Cabopino is sometimes a few cents cheaper. That premium of roughly 25-30% over road diesel is the underlying reason surcharges exist. Operators either absorb it (and price it into the headline rate) or pass it through. How much fuel a Marbella charter actually burns Consumption depends on hull type and speed. Rough figures for the kind of boats you see in Puerto Banús: Boat type Cruise speed Litres / hour Sailing catamaran (Lagoon 380) 6-8 kts under engine 4-8 Small RIB / speedboat (7m) 20-25 kts 20-35 12m planing motor yacht (Azimut 39) 18-22 kts 80-120 14-18m flybridge (Pershing 46, Azimut 58) 22-26 kts 150-260 24-29m superyacht (Mangusta 80, Ferretti 94) 22-28 kts 250-450 A four-hour trip on an Azimut 39 cruising mostly at 20 kts will therefore burn roughly 350-450 litres. At €1.70/L that is €595-€765 of fuel. You can see why operators on tight margins want this off their own balance sheet. How our pricing avoids the surcharge We build an average consumption figure into the headline rate based on a typical Marbella itinerary — a mix of cruise, drift, swim stops and a short blast back to port. For our Tier A boats (Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39), that lands at €749 for two hours rising to €2,299 for a full eight hours , fuel included. For the Mangusta 80 it is €4,719 for a four-hour minimum , with a jet ski thrown in free. This works because we run our own boats from Puerto Banús and know what each itinerary actually costs. We are not reselling someone else's hull and trying to predict their fuel bill. For peak-season nuance see our note on August chartering . What is included alongside fuel Licensed Spanish skipper for the whole charter Soft drinks, beer, white wine and cava on ice Light snacks — olives, crisps, fruit Full insurance and safety equipment Spanish IVA at 21% Port departure and re-entry fees at Puerto Banús The only thing not included is a tip for the skipper if you enjoyed the day, and any food beyond light snacks (we can arrange catering — see our dietary guide ). Other hidden fees to watch for elsewhere If you are comparing quotes from multiple operators, ask specifically about: port re-entry fees (sometimes €40-€80 extra at peak times), end-of-day cleaning fees (€60-€150), ice and drink top-ups, IVA shown separately at checkout, and "skipper gratuity included" wording that is then re-requested in cash. A €600 advertised half-day can stack to €900+ once these add up. We list one number on WhatsApp and that is the number you pay. Sail vs motor: the fuel maths If keeping fuel costs (or environmental footprint) low matters to you, a sailing catamaran is the obvious choice. Our Lagoon 380 uses 4-8 L/h under engine and zero under sail. A summer afternoon from Puerto Banús to the anchorage off Río Verde and back might burn 15-20 litres total. Even at full surcharge pricing, that is negligible — but with us it is already in the rate. Motor yachts are faster and more comfortable in chop, which is why most Marbella guests still choose them. The trade-off is fuel. Our all-inclusive pricing simply removes that variable from the decision. Getting a surcharge-free quote Message us on WhatsApp with your date, group size and what you want to do — a sunset cruise along the Golden Mile, a full-day run to Sotogrande, a swim-and-snorkel afternoon off Cala del Faro. We reply with the all-in euro figure, confirm the boat from our Puerto Banús fleet , and hold the slot. No fuel reconciliation invoice the next morning. Frequently asked questions What is a yacht fuel surcharge in Puerto Banús? A fuel surcharge is an extra fee added on top of the advertised charter price to cover the diesel or petrol consumed during your trip. On the Costa del Sol it typically runs 20-40% of the base hire, sometimes calculated by litres burned, sometimes as a flat percentage. Our charters do not use this model — fuel is included in the headline rate, so the price you see on WhatsApp is the price you pay. How much does marine diesel cost in Puerto Banús in 2026? Marine diesel at Puerto Banús and nearby ports sits around €1.55-€1.85 per litre in 2026, depending on the supplier and whether you fuel at the pontoon or via bunker delivery. That is roughly 25-30% above road diesel because marine fuel is taxed and handled differently. This is why traditional operators pass the cost through as a surcharge rather than absorbing it. How much fuel does a typical Marbella charter burn? A 12m planing motor yacht like an Azimut 39 burns roughly 80-120 litres per hour cruising at 18-22 knots, and 25-40 L/h at displacement speed. Bigger flybridge yachts in the 24-29m range can burn 200-400 L/h at cruise. Sailing catamarans like our Lagoon 380 sip 4-8 L/h under engine and zero under sail, which is why sail charters rarely carry surcharges. Do your prices include fuel? Yes. Every charter we list includes fuel, a licensed skipper, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety kit and Spanish IVA at 21%. The €749/2h Tier A rate and €4,719/4h Mangusta 80 rate are final — no fuel surcharge, no port fee bolt-on, no service charge added at the pontoon. See our pricing breakdown for peak-season context. Why do some operators still charge fuel separately? Two reasons. First, it lets them advertise a low headline price to win clicks, then recover margin at the dock. Second, fuel use genuinely varies — a flat-water cruise to Cala del Faro uses far less than a fast run to Sotogrande into a westerly. We solve this by building an average consumption figure into the rate, which works because our skippers know the routes and weather windows. Can I ask the skipper to slow down to save fuel? On a surcharge-based charter, yes — and many guests do, because every litre saved comes off their invoice. On our all-inclusive charters there is no financial incentive either way, so you can ask the skipper to run fast to Estepona for lunch or drift slowly along the Golden Mile without watching a fuel gauge. The price stays the same. Are there other hidden fees on Puerto Banús charters? Home › Guide › Puerto Banus Yacht Rental in Winter: Why Off-Season Works By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Most visitors assume Puerto Banus yacht rental is a summer-only affair.
