Why Winter Works for Puerto Banus Yacht Rental The Costa del Sol enjoys a mild microclimate thanks to the Sierra Blanca mountains. Puerto Banus remains fully operational — restaurants, bars, and shops stay open, and the marina is quieter, so you'll get a berth without fuss. URL: https://boatrentalinmarbella.com/blog/seasickness-kids-marbella-boat/
TITLE: Seasickness in Kids on a Marbella Boat: Prevent &amp; Treat
DESCRIPTION: Prevent and treat seasickness in kids on a Marbella boat charter. Expert tips, remedies, and what to pack for a smooth family trip from Puerto Banús.

Home › Guide › Seasickness in Kids on a Marbella Boat: Prevent & Treat By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Seasickness can turn a dream family day on the water into a miserable experience. But with the right preparation, you can keep your kids comfortable and happy during a boat charter from Marbella. This guide covers prevention, natural remedies, medication, and what to do if symptoms strike – so your family can enjoy the Costa del Sol from the sea. Why children get seasick on boats in Marbella Seasickness happens when the brain receives mixed signals from the eyes (which see a stable cabin) and the inner ear (which feels the boat's motion). Children under 12 are more prone because their vestibular system is still maturing. The gentle swell off Puerto Banús can trigger nausea even on calm days. Motion on smaller boats like the Speedboat (7m, 6 pax) or Dubhe (8m, 5 pax) is more pronounced, while larger vessels like the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) offer greater stability. Before you board: prevention strategies The best cure is prevention. Start 24 hours before your charter: ensure your child sleeps well and eats lightly. On the morning of your trip, serve a bland breakfast – toast, banana, or plain cereal – and avoid dairy or fried foods. Hydrate with water, not juice. Consider giving an age-appropriate antihistamine 30-60 minutes before departure. Dimenhydrinate (Dramamine) is common for children over 2, but always consult a pharmacist. Ginger chews or crystallised ginger are a natural alternative and can be offered 20 minutes before boarding. What to pack for a seasickness-free day Your packing list should include: ginger chews or lollipops, acupressure wristbands (Sea-Bands), a small towel, extra water, and salty snacks like pretzels or crackers. Avoid sugary drinks – our charters include still water, soft drinks, and light snacks. A light jacket or windbreaker helps if the breeze is cool. If your child is prone to motion sickness, pack a small bag for emergencies. Our boats have heads (toilets) on board, so access is easy. Onboard tips to minimise motion sickness Once aboard, choose a seat in the centre of the boat on the lower deck – this is where motion is least. Have your child face forward and focus on the horizon or a fixed point on land, like La Concha mountain. Avoid reading, tablets, or phones. Keep them cool with a hat and sunscreen. If the boat is anchored for swimming, the motion stops, which can help reset the inner ear. Our skippers are experienced with families and can adjust the route to calmer waters near Cabopino or Estepona. What to do if seasickness strikes If your child feels queasy, act quickly. Move them to the centre of the boat, lower deck. Have them focus on the horizon. Offer small sips of water or ginger ale. Do not let them lie down with eyes closed – that often worsens the sensation. If vomiting occurs, clean up calmly and offer fresh air. A cool cloth on the forehead helps. Most children recover within 20 minutes once they are stable. If symptoms persist, the skipper can return to port early – all our charters are flexible. Choosing the right boat for kids Bigger boats mean less motion. For families, the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) at €749/2h or the Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) are excellent choices. The Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) offers supreme stability and includes a jet ski for older kids. Avoid smaller speedboats if motion is a concern. All our boats depart from Puerto Banús and cruise along the sheltered coast, minimising rough conditions. Natural remedies that work Ginger is the most researched natural treatment for nausea. Ginger chews, capsules, or even ginger tea (cooled) can be given to children. Acupressure wristbands (Sea-Bands) apply pressure to the P6 point on the wrist and are safe for kids of all ages. Peppermint oil on a tissue or a peppermint sweet can also soothe the stomach. These are best used alongside the prevention tips above, not as a substitute for medication if your child is highly susceptible. When to see a doctor before your trip If your child has a history of severe motion sickness, or if they are under 2, speak to your paediatrician before the charter. They may recommend a prescription patch (scopolamine) for older children or advise on safe antihistamine dosing. For most kids, over-the-counter options and natural remedies are sufficient. Our charters are family-friendly, and we can recommend the calmest times of day – typically morning departures from Puerto Banús. Frequently asked questions What causes seasickness in children on a boat in Marbella? Seasickness occurs when the brain receives conflicting signals from the inner ear (motion) and eyes (stillness). On a boat in Marbella, gentle rolling or choppy waves off Puerto Banús can trigger nausea. Children under 12 are more susceptible because their balance systems are still developing. How can I prevent seasickness in my child before a Marbella boat trip? Give your child a light meal 1-2 hours before departure – avoid greasy or heavy foods. Consider an antihistamine like dimenhydrinate (Dramamine) 30-60 minutes before boarding, but check with a pharmacist for age-appropriate dosing. Ginger chews or crystallised ginger also help settle the stomach naturally. What should I pack for my child to avoid seasickness on a Marbella charter? Pack ginger chews or lollipops, acupressure wristbands (Sea-Bands), a small towel, extra water, and salty snacks like pretzels. Avoid sugary drinks – our charter includes still water and soft drinks. A light jacket or windbreaker helps if the breeze is cool. Are there any medications safe for children with seasickness on a boat? Yes, dimenhydrinate (Dramamine) is commonly used for children over 2 years – follow the weight-based dosing on the label. Meclizine (Bonine) is another option but is typically for children over 12. Always consult a paediatrician or pharmacist before giving any medication, especially for toddlers. Which Marbella boat charter is best for kids prone to seasickness? Larger, heavier boats like the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) or Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) offer greater stability in calm Marbella waters. The Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) is also a good choice for smaller groups. Avoid smaller speedboats like the Mariah SX21 (6.4m) if motion is a concern. What should I do if my child starts feeling seasick during the charter? Move your child to the centre of the boat where motion is least, ideally on the lower deck. Have them focus on the horizon or a fixed point on land. Offer small sips of water or ginger ale. Avoid letting them lie down with eyes closed – that can worsen the sensation. If vomiting occurs, clean up and offer fresh air. Are there natural remedies for seasickness in kids that work? Ginger is the most studied natural remedy – ginger chews, capsules, or tea can reduce nausea. Acupressure wristbands (Sea-Bands) apply pressure to the P6 point on the wrist and are safe for children. Peppermint oil on a tissue can also help calm the stomach. These are best used alongside prevention strategies. Can I book a shorter charter to test if my child gets seasick? Absolutely. Our Tier A boats start from €749 for a 2-hour charter, which is enough time to cruise from Puerto Banús to Cala del Faro or along the Golden Mile. If your child feels fine, you can extend to a longer trip next time. TITLE: How to Avoid Seasickness on a Marbella Charter (Captain&#x27;s Guide)
DESCRIPTION: Captain&#x27;s guide to avoiding seasickness on a Marbella boat charter: pick a catamaran, take ginger or Stugeron 1h before boarding, stare at the horizon, avoid below-decks.

Home › Guide › How to Avoid Seasickness on a Marbella Charter (Captain's Guide) By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Seasickness ruins more Marbella charters than weather does. The good news: it's almost entirely preventable. This page is the captain's-perspective playbook — boat choice, what to take, what to eat, how to behave on board, and what to do if it starts anyway. Step 1: pick the right boat Stability differs more between boat types than anything else you can control. Ranked from most-stable (least-seasick-inducing) to least: Power catamaran — two hulls, flat deck, twin engines, no heel. Best. Sail catamaran — same flat deck, but rolling at anchor and slightly slower transit. Large motor yacht 16 m+ — mass dampens motion. Good. Standard motor yacht 10-14 m — fine for most people in normal sea. Sportfisher — narrow beam, deeper roll. Worse at anchor. Sailing monohull — heels 15-20° under sail. The classic seasickness boat. Licence-free 5 m runabout — light, every wave is felt. If anyone in the group has a history of seasickness, book a catamaran rental Marbella and the issue is largely solved before you start. Step 2: medication The two over-the-counter preventatives that actually work: Stugeron (cinnarizine 15mg) — Spanish pharmacy €5-8, UK pharmacy without prescription. Take 1 hour before boarding. 6-8 hour cover. Slight drowsiness possible. The default choice. Scopolamine patch (Transderm Scop) — behind-the-ear patch, prescription only, lasts 72 hours. Reserve for multi-day charters or known severe sufferers. What to skip: Dramamine (older, more drowsy), pressure bracelets (placebo for most, can't hurt), homeopathic anything (does nothing). Ginger — raw ginger, capsules or strong tea before boarding — helps roughly 30% of people. Cheap, no side effects, worth trying alongside Stugeron, not instead of it. Step 3: food before boarding Eat something — empty stomach + motion = nausea even in stable people. Light breakfast: bread, banana, eggs, yogurt. Avoid: alcohol the night before, fried food the morning of, strong coffee on an empty stomach, anything spicy. On board: sip water, nibble dry crackers or apple if you feel queasy. Avoid fatty snacks and dairy until back on land. Step 4: behaviour on board Stay topside. The cabin amplifies motion and removes the horizon — the worst place to be when queasy. Stare at the horizon. Your inner ear feels motion; your eyes need a matching visual reference. The horizon delivers exactly that. Stand or sit in the middle of the boat — least motion. The bow and the stern move more. Face forward. Looking sideways or backwards is worse for the vestibular system. Don't read or use your phone — the brain rebels at static text while the world moves. Fresh air — keep the wind on your face. Diesel fumes near the stern can tip a borderline stomach. Step 5: when it starts anyway Early signs: yawning, sweating, sudden cool feeling, slight saliva increase, withdrawal from conversation. Act fast: Tell the skipper. They'll adjust speed and heading, or head for a calmer anchor. Move to the middle of the boat, sit low, eyes on horizon. Sip cold water with a slice of lemon. Chew a piece of ginger or take a second Stugeron if you took the first one less than 4 hours ago. If vomiting: do it over the leeward side (skipper will indicate). Hydrate after. Most people recover within 30 minutes once anchored in calm water. The trip can usually continue. If you're a known sufferer Book a power catamaran, take Stugeron, eat a small breakfast, pick a flat-sea morning (early start beats afternoon levante), and you'll have a fine day. Mention seasickness when booking and the skipper will design the route to favour calm water. For more on which boat suits whom, the boat rental Marbella hub compares every option. Frequently asked questions Does seasickness wear off? For most people, yes — within 24-48 hours of continuous exposure your inner ear adapts. A 4-hour day charter ends before that kicks in, so prevention beats cure on a short trip. What's the single best preventative? Stugeron (cinnarizine 15mg) taken one hour before boarding. Effective for 6-8 hours. Combine with picking a catamaran and you've eliminated 90% of risk. Will the skipper turn around if I'm sick? Yes — every Marbella skipper will end a trip early or head to the calmest anchor. Tell them as soon as you feel queasy; don't wait. Are pressure bracelets (sea-bands) effective? Placebo effect for most people, but the placebo is real and they can't hurt. Cinnarizine (Stugeron) has actual pharmacological evidence behind it; sea-bands don't. Combine both if you like; rely on the medication. Can I drink alcohol if I'm prone to seasickness? Skip alcohol for 12 hours before and during the early part of the charter. Alcohol disrupts the vestibular system's balance signals and amplifies motion sensitivity. Once you're settled at anchor in calm water and feeling fine, a glass of cava is acceptable; until then, water only. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

Home › Guide › Semana Santa in Marbella by Boat: Quiet-Side Cruises Between Processions By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Semana Santa in Marbella is a sensory overload of incense, drumbeats, and solemn processions winding through the Old Town. A Semana Santa yacht Marbella charter lets you escape the cobblestone crush for a few hours of sun, sea, and silence. Depart from Puerto Banús, anchor in a hidden cove, and return in time for the evening procession. Our fleet of 29 boats starts at €749 for 2 hours, all inclusive. Why Combine a Boat Charter with Semana Santa? Marbella’s processions are concentrated in the historic centre and along the Golden Mile. The town swells with visitors, making it hard to find a quiet moment. A private boat offers a literal escape hatch. Within 15 minutes of leaving Puerto Banús, you’re alone with the sea. You can swim, sunbathe, or simply watch the coast from a distance. And because our charters include a skipper, fuel, drinks, and snacks, you don’t lift a finger. Just step aboard and exhale. Quiet Anchorages Within 30 Minutes of Puerto Banús Cala del Faro – 5 NM west, rocky cliffs, clear water, rarely crowded. Cala Cortés – 8 NM east, sheltered bay with a small beach. TITLE: Sotogrande vs Puerto Banus: Marina Comparison 2026
DESCRIPTION: Sotogrande marina vs Puerto Banus: compare facilities, dining, and boat charter access in 2026. Find which Costa del Sol port suits your yacht trip best.

Home › Guide › Sotogrande vs Puerto Banus: Marina Comparison 2026 By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Choosing between Sotogrande marina vs Puerto Banus for your Costa del Sol yacht charter in 2026? Both ports are excellent, but they serve different tastes. Puerto Banús is the glitzy, non-stop social hub with 915 berths, designer shops, and world-class dining. Sotogrande is a quieter, family-oriented resort marina with 452 berths, set within a 4,000-acre estate. This comparison covers facilities, dining, accessibility, and how each connects to our boat charter fleet departing from Puerto Banús. Location and Access Puerto Banús sits 6 km west of Marbella town, right on the Golden Mile. It's a 15-minute taxi from Marbella centre (€15–20). Sotogrande is 20 km west of Estepona, about 35 minutes by car from Marbella. By sea, the distance is 20 nautical miles — roughly 25 minutes in a powerboat like our Astondoa 40 . Both marinas have good road access via the A-7 motorway, but Sotogrande is less congested, especially in summer. Facilities and Berthing Puerto Banús offers 915 berths for yachts up to 50 m, plus a 200 m commercial dock. Services include 24-hour security, fuel dock, pump-out, and a chandlery. Sotogrande has 452 berths for yachts up to 30 m, with a smaller fuel dock and fewer services. For mega-yachts, Puerto Banús is the clear choice — our Ferretti 94 (29 m) fits easily there. Sotogrande is better for smaller craft and has a more relaxed, less industrial feel. Dining and Nightlife Puerto Banús has over 60 restaurants, from Michelin-starred Skina to casual beachfront chiringuitos. Nightlife is legendary — Pangea and Aqwa are open until 5 am. Sotogrande has about 15 restaurants within the marina, including the excellent El Churrasco and the family-friendly La Terraza. For a quiet dinner with sunset views, Sotogrande wins. For a party, Puerto Banús is unmatched. Both have excellent seafood, but Puerto Banús has more variety. Boat Charter Access All our boats depart from Puerto Banús. This includes our popular Azimut 39 (11 pax) and the Mangusta 80 (12 pax) with a free jet ski. If you're staying in Sotogrande, we can arrange a taxi transfer (€45–60 one way) or a marina pickup for an additional fee — contact us via WhatsApp for details. Most guests find the 25-minute drive from Sotogrande to Puerto Banús quick and easy. Family-Friendliness Sotogrande is designed for families: it has a golf course, tennis courts, a beach club, and wide pedestrian walkways safe for children. Puerto Banús has a playground and kid-friendly restaurants, but the crowds and late-night noise can be overwhelming. For a family day charter, we recommend departing from Puerto Banús and heading west to Cala del Faro or Cabopino — both are quiet anchorages with shallow waters for kids. Cost and Value Berthing fees in Puerto Banús are higher — expect €200–400 per night for a 12 m boat in summer. Sotogrande is about 20–30% cheaper. Dining costs are comparable: a meal for two with wine is €80–150 in either marina. For charter guests, our pricing is the same regardless of marina — see our fleet rates . Tier A boats like the Astondoa 40 start at €749 for 2 hours, all inclusive. Weather and Sea Conditions Both marinas enjoy the same Mediterranean climate: 300+ sunny days per year, average 22°C water in August. The Strait of Gibraltar's levante wind can affect Sotogrande more than Puerto Banús, which is sheltered by the Sierra Blanca mountains. For a smoother charter, depart from Puerto Banús and head east towards Cabopino or Cala Cortés. Frequently asked questions Which marina is bigger, Sotogrande or Puerto Banus? Puerto Banús is larger with 915 berths and a 45,000 m² shopping area. Sotogrande marina has 452 berths but is set within a 4,000-acre resort with golf courses and a beach club. Puerto Banús feels busier and more commercial; Sotogrande is more intimate. Is Sotogrande marina quieter than Puerto Banus? Yes, Sotogrande is noticeably quieter, especially outside summer weekends. Puerto Banús is lively year-round, with peak crowds from June to September. If you prefer a relaxed evening with fewer people, Sotogrande is better. Can I charter a boat from Sotogrande marina? Our fleet departs exclusively from Puerto Banús, not Sotogrande. However, we can arrange pickup at Sotogrande for an additional fee (contact us via WhatsApp for pricing). Most guests find the 25-minute transfer from Sotogrande to Puerto Banús convenient. Which marina has better restaurants and nightlife? Puerto Banús has over 60 restaurants, including Michelin-starred options like Skina, and lively nightclubs like Pangea. Sotogrande has about 15 restaurants within the marina, more family-oriented, with a quieter bar scene. For nightlife, Puerto Banús wins. What are the parking facilities like at each marina? Puerto Banús has large underground car parks (€3–5/hour) but can be congested in summer. Sotogrande offers ample free parking close to the marina. If you're driving, Sotogrande is more convenient. Can I walk from Puerto Banus to Marbella town? It's a 6 km walk along the coastal path, taking about 1 hour 15 minutes. Many people prefer taking a taxi (€15–20) or the local bus (€1.50). Sotogrande is 20 km from Estepona, so a car or taxi is necessary. Which marina is better for families with children? Sotogrande is more family-friendly with its golf resort, tennis courts, and the Almenara beach club. Puerto Banús has a playground and kid-friendly restaurants, but the crowds and nightlife can be less suitable for young children after dark. Are pets allowed at Sotogrande and Puerto Banus marinas? Both marinas allow well-behaved dogs on leads. Puerto Banús has more pet-friendly restaurants with outdoor seating. Sotogrande's wider walkways are easier for dog walks. Always check with individual venues. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

Faster boats like the Mangusta 80 (24m) can cover it in 35 minutes, while a more relaxed Azimut 39 (12.5m) might take 45–50 minutes. Sea conditions are usually calm from May to October, with swell rarely exceeding 0.5 metres. If you add a swimming stop at Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés, plan for an extra 20–30 minutes. TITLE: Sotogrande Yacht Charter Fishing Trip: Species &amp; Seasons
DESCRIPTION: Plan a sotogrande yacht charter fishing trip with our expert guide to species &amp; seasons. Book a boat from Puerto Banús for bluefin tuna, seabass &amp; more.

Home › Guide › Sotogrande Yacht Charter Fishing Trip: Species & Seasons By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers A sotogrande yacht charter fishing trip is one of the most rewarding ways to experience the Costa del Sol’s rich marine life. While Sotogrande itself is a prestigious marina, our fleet departs from Puerto Banús, just 20 nautical miles north — a 45-minute cruise south along the coast. Whether you’re after bluefin tuna, seabass, or sea bream, the waters off Sotogrande offer excellent fishing from spring through autumn. Here’s what you need to know about species, seasons, and booking the right boat. Why fish near Sotogrande? Sotogrande sits at the mouth of the Guadiaro River, where freshwater meets the Mediterranean. This nutrient-rich zone attracts baitfish, which in turn draw larger predators. The seabed drops quickly to 50–100 metres just a few miles offshore, creating ideal conditions for trolling and bottom fishing. Unlike the crowded beaches of Marbella, the waters off Sotogrande are quieter, with less boat traffic and clearer water. For a sotogrande yacht charter fishing trip , you’ll want a boat that can cover the distance comfortably and give you deck space to fight fish. Our recommended boats for fishing All our charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and insurance. For fishing, we suggest boats with speed and space. The Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) from €4,719 for 4 hours includes a free jet ski — perfect for families who want both fishing and water sports. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) offers a large cockpit and flybridge for spotting fish. For smaller groups, the Pershing 46 (14m, 10 pax) is fast and nimble. All depart from Puerto Banús. For a full list, see our boat rental Marbella fleet. Boat Length Pax Price from Best for Mangusta 80 24m 12 €4,719/4h Trolling & jet ski Azimut 58 Flybridge 17.6m 12 On request Flybridge viewing Pershing 46 14m 10 On request Speed & agility Astondoa 40 12.5m 9 €749/2h Budget-friendly Species by season The Mediterranean off Sotogrande hosts a variety of game fish. Here’s a seasonal breakdown: Spring (March–May): Seabass and sea bream are abundant inshore. Water temps 15–20°C. Good for light tackle fishing. Summer (June–September): Bluefin tuna arrive, often in schools. Water temps 22–28°C. Trolling with lures is most effective. Also bonito and mackerel. Autumn (October–November): Seabass return as waters cool. Amberjack and dentex are possible in deeper areas. Water temps 18–22°C. Winter (December–February): Slower fishing, but hake and red mullet can be caught. Most charters run year-round. For a serious sotogrande yacht charter fishing trip , target June–September for tuna or March–May for seabass. Check our best time to charter a boat in Marbella guide for more detail. What’s included in your charter Every booking includes a licensed skipper who knows the local waters, fuel for the day, and a selection of drinks (water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava) plus light snacks. Insurance and safety equipment are covered. Spanish IVA (21%) is included in all prices. Fishing gear is not provided, but you can bring your own rods and tackle. The skipper will help with rigging and recommend bait. For larger groups, the Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) offers luxury and space, but pricing is on request. Planning your trip: distance and timing From Puerto Banús to Sotogrande is roughly 20 nautical miles. At 25 knots, that’s 45 minutes each way. For a full fishing day, we recommend an 8-hour charter (€2,299 for Tier A boats, or Tier B from €4,719/4h). A 6-hour trip gives you about 4 hours of actual fishing time. Half-day (4h) options are available but limit you to inshore grounds near Cabopino or Cala del Faro. Always factor in travel time when booking. Our skippers will suggest the best route based on conditions and target species. Where to fish near Sotogrande Popular fishing grounds include the area off Cabo de Gata (rocky reefs), the Guadiaro River mouth (seabass), and deeper waters south of Estepona (tuna). The coastline between Puerto Banús and Sotogrande offers varied underwater topography — sand flats, seagrass meadows, and rocky outcrops. Your skipper will adjust based on wind and tide. For a truly memorable day, combine fishing with a stop at Cala Cortés for a swim and lunch. See our Marbella boat charter itineraries for route ideas. Frequently asked questions Can I fish from any of your boats? Yes, all our boats are suitable for rod fishing. The skipper will advise on the best spots. For serious trolling, we recommend the Mangusta 80 (24m) or Azimut 58 Flybridge for their speed and deck space. Do you provide fishing equipment? We do not supply rods or tackle as standard. You are welcome to bring your own gear. The skipper can help with rigging and bait. If you need rental equipment, please ask when booking and we can arrange it locally. What is the best time of year for bluefin tuna? Bluefin tuna are most common from June to September when sea temperatures reach 22–28°C. July and August offer the highest catch rates. We recommend a full-day charter (8 hours) to maximise your chances. How far is Sotogrande from Puerto Banús? Sotogrande is about 20 nautical miles south of Puerto Banús. By powerboat at 25–30 knots, it takes roughly 45 minutes. Our boats depart from Puerto Banús, so factor this travel time into your charter. Can I keep the fish I catch? Yes, you may keep legal-sized fish within Spanish regulations. The skipper will advise on size limits and bag limits. Many guests choose catch-and-release, especially for bluefin tuna. We can help with cleaning if needed. What other fish can I catch near Sotogrande? Common catches include seabass, sea bream, amberjack, mackerel, and bonito. In deeper waters off Cabo de Gata, you may also find dentex and grouper. The variety is excellent from spring through autumn. Is a fishing licence required? No separate licence is needed for recreational fishing on a charter boat with a licensed skipper. The boat's insurance covers all passengers. Just bring your own rod or rent one locally. What is the minimum charter duration for fishing? We recommend at least 6 hours to reach productive grounds and have enough fishing time. An 8-hour charter gives you the best chance, especially for tuna. TITLE: Speedboat: Best Boat in Marbella for Tow-sports and watersports day
DESCRIPTION: Why a speedboat is best for tow-sports and watersports in Marbella. 6 pax, 7m, departs Puerto Banús. Book your day of action now.

Home › Guide › Speedboat: Best Boat in Marbella for Tow-sports and watersports day By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers If you are looking for the speedboat best for tow-sports and watersports in Marbella, our 7-metre, 6-passenger speedboat is the clear choice. Departing from Puerto Banús, this agile boat combines a powerful engine with a shallow draft, letting you zip into sheltered coves and pull a skier or tuber with ease. Whether you want to learn to wakeboard or just blast around the coast, this is the boat for an action-packed day on the water. Why a speedboat is the best choice for watersports Not every charter boat is built for tow-sports. A speedboat's lightweight hull and responsive steering give you the quick acceleration and tight turning needed for waterskiing, wakeboarding, and tubing. Our speedboat is purpose-designed for this kind of fun – it gets on plane fast, holds a steady wake, and handles like a sports car. Compare that to a heavy flybridge or motor yacht, which can feel sluggish and create a wash that is less forgiving for beginners. Specs and capacity Length: 7 metres Maximum passengers: 6 (including skipper) Departure point: Puerto Banús, Marbella Engine power: Sufficient for waterskiing and wakeboarding at planing speeds Draft: Shallow – ideal for accessing coves like Cala del Faro and Cala Cortés With a maximum of 6 people on board, your group gets plenty of time on the water without feeling crowded. The skipper handles all navigation and safety, so you can focus on the fun. Tow-sports equipment – what you need to know Tow-sports equipment is not included in the base charter price, but we can arrange hire for waterskis, wakeboards, tubes, kneeboards, and life jackets. Just let us know what you need when you book via WhatsApp. Prices vary depending on the equipment and duration. We recommend booking at least 48 hours in advance to guarantee availability. Best coves and spots for watersports near Marbella The Costa del Sol coastline offers plenty of sheltered waters perfect for tow-sports. From Puerto Banús, your skipper can take you to: Cala del Faro – a quiet cove with flat water, great for beginners Cala Cortés – slightly larger, with good depth for wakeboarding Cabopino – a sandy bay with a protected inner area Estepona – calmer waters further west, often less busy The speedboat's shallow draft means you can get closer to shore than larger yachts, giving you access to these sheltered spots even in light wind. TITLE: Speedboat Charter Review: What It&#x27;s Like Onboard in Marbella
DESCRIPTION: Honest speedboat Marbella review: what it&#x27;s like onboard our 7m speedboat in Puerto Banús. € pricing, top speed, swim stops &amp; who it suits best.

Home › Guide › Speedboat Charter Review: What It's Like Onboard in Marbella By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers If you’re looking for a fast, fun, and affordable way to get on the water in Marbella, our 7m speedboat might be exactly what you need. This is a straight-talking review of what it’s actually like onboard – no fluff, just the facts from a real charter experience. We’ll cover the boat itself, the route, the vibe, and who it’s best suited for. First Impressions: The Boat Our 7m speedboat is a sporty open-bow design, white hull, blue upholstery. It’s not a luxury yacht – it’s a day boat built for speed and sun. There’s a small windscreen, a helm station with basic electronics, and seating for six across two bench seats. The foredeck has a sunpad area for lounging. No cabin, no toilet, no galley – just a cooler with drinks and snacks provided. The boat feels solid and well-maintained, with a Yamaha outboard that pushes it to around 35 knots in good conditions. Departure from Puerto Banús All our speedboat charters depart from Puerto Banús , Marbella’s most famous marina. You meet the skipper at the appointed time, board at the pontoon, and get a quick safety briefing. The skipper will ask about your preferences – fast cruising, relaxed sightseeing, swimming stops. Within five minutes you’re motoring past the superyacht row, heading out past the breakwater into the Mediterranean. The sound of the engine changes as you open the throttle, and the bow lifts. It’s an immediate adrenaline hit. Onboard Experience: Speed, Sound & Comfort At cruising speed (around 25 knots) the ride is firm but not jarring – the hull handles chop well for a 7m boat. At full throttle you’ll feel the wind and spray; bring a windbreaker even on sunny days. The seats are comfortable for up to an hour at a time, but there’s no shade beyond the small windscreen. The skipper will adjust speed to suit the group. The sound level is loud enough that conversation requires raised voices, but not deafening. It’s part of the experience – raw and exciting. Typical Route & Swim Stops A common route goes west from Puerto Banús towards Marbella Marina , then on to Cala del Faro – a small cove with clear water and rocky cliffs. Alternatively, heading east takes you to Cabopino and the Artola Dunes . The skipper will anchor in a calm spot for swimming. The boat has a telescopic ladder at the stern for easy reboarding. Snorkelling gear isn’t provided, so bring your own if you want to explore underwater. Typical swim stops last 20–40 minutes depending on your itinerary. Who Is This Boat For? This speedboat is ideal for small groups of friends, couples, or families with older children (life jackets are available for all ages). It’s not a luxury yacht experience – it’s about the thrill of the ride, the sun, and the sea. If you want a quiet, lounging day with a full galley and air conditioning, look at our larger motor yachts like the Azimut 58 or Mangusta 80. If you want a fast, affordable half-day charter with real speed, this is your boat. Pricing & Booking Pricing for the speedboat is on request – there’s no fixed rate because each charter is tailored to duration and route. Contact us via WhatsApp for a quote. The price includes the skipper, fuel, insurance, drinks (water, soft drinks, beer, wine, cava), light snacks, and the 21% Spanish VAT. No hidden fees. A typical half-day (4 hours) is very competitive compared to larger boats in our fleet. For comparison, our Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39) start at €749 for 2 hours – the speedboat is significantly less. What’s Not Included No meals, no snorkel gear, no towels, no toilet. Plan ahead: use the marina facilities before departure, bring sunscreen, a towel, and a waterproof bag for valuables. The skipper will advise on the best public stops if needed. Final Verdict The 7m speedboat delivers exactly what it promises: a fast, fun, no-frills day on the water in Marbella. It’s not for everyone – if you want luxury, book a yacht charter . If you want speed, sun, and swim stops at a fair price, this is an excellent choice. The skipper makes it safe and easy, and departing from Puerto Banús adds instant glamour. For a 3–4 hour adventure, it’s hard to beat. Frequently Asked Questions How fast does the speedboat go? Our 7m speedboat reaches around 35 knots depending on load and sea conditions. It’s quick enough for a thrill but comfortable for cruising – not a race boat. Can I drive the speedboat myself? No. A licensed skipper is included with every charter. You can ask to take the wheel under supervision if conditions allow, but the skipper remains in control. Is there a toilet onboard? No, the speedboat has no cabin or toilet. We plan stops at beaches or marinas with public facilities, so it’s manageable for a half-day trip. What’s included in the price? The charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, insurance, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, and Spanish VAT (21%). No hidden extras. Where can we go on the speedboat? Typical routes head east to Cabopino or west to Marbella Marina and Cala del Faro. The skipper can tailor the trip to your preferences, weather permitting. How many people fit on the speedboat? Maximum 6 guests plus the skipper. It’s best for 4–5 adults for comfort, though 6 works for a short trip. Life jackets are provided for all. Can we swim and snorkel? Yes. The skipper will stop at calm coves like Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés for swimming. We provide a ladder for easy reboarding. How do I book the speedboat? Pricing is on request – message us on WhatsApp. We’ll confirm availability, give you a fixed price, and reserve the boat. A deposit secures the booking. Ready to book?
